```
Perfect! I can see the issue now. Let me create a summary document with all the findings:

## FUSE Pipeline Trace - Complete Analysis

I've traced the entire FUSE write → upload → publish pipeline through the codebase. Here's the complete call chain with the critical bug identified:

### **COMPLETE CALL CHAIN**

#### **1. FILE CREATION** 
- **Entry:** `operations.rs:228` - `create()` delegates to `write_ops::handle_create()`
- **Handler:** `write_ops.rs:126-247`
  - Line 154: Allocate new inode with `InodeKind::File`
  - Line 176-183: Generate random Ed25519 IPNS keypair for file
  - Line 215: Insert inode into table
  - Line 225-241: Create writable OpenFileHandle from temp file
  - Line 243: **Mark parent folder as mutated:** `fs.mutated_folders.insert(parent, Instant::now())`

#### **2. FILE WRITE**
- **Entry:** `operations.rs:236` - `write()` delegates to `write_ops::handle_write()`
- **Handler:** `write_ops.rs:90-123`
  - Line 98-104: Get open file handle
  - Line 106-117: Write data to handle's temp file on disk
  - Line 109-114: Update inode size/mtime in memory

#### **3. FILE RELEASE (CLOSE)**
- **Entry:** `operations.rs:244` - `release()` delegates to `read_ops::handle_release()`
- **Handler:** `read_ops.rs:656-855` - **THIS IS WHERE THE CRITICAL DRAINING HAPPENS**
  - Line 662: **Drain upload completions** `fs.drain_upload_completions()` - picks up any previous uploads
  - Line 673-674: Check if file needs upload (dirty or new)
  - Line 677-844: **Spawn background upload thread** (NOT on FUSE thread)
    - Line 799-801: Upload encrypted content to IPFS → get back `file_cid`
    - Line 805-812: **Send UploadComplete message** via `upload_tx` channel
    - Line 814-833: If file IPNS key available, call `publish_file_metadata()` for per-file IPNS publish
  - Line 851: Clean up temp file

#### **4. UPLOAD COMPLETION HANDLING**
- **Received by:** `UploadComplete` message arrives on `upload_rx` channel
- **Drain location:** `lib.rs:599-618` - `drain_upload_completions()`
  - Line 600: Non-blocking `try_recv()` on channel
  - Line 601-603: Update inode's CID with uploaded file's CID
  - Line 615: **Decrement pending_uploads counter** in `publish_queue` for parent folder
  - Line 617: **Call flush_publish_queue()** to check if publish should fire

#### **5. FOLDER METADATA PUBLISH**
- **Triggered by:** `lib.rs:626-640` - `flush_publish_queue()`
  - Line 630-632: Filter publish_queue for entries ready to publish:
    - Safety valve: 10s elapsed since first_dirty
    - OR: 1.5s debounce + NO pending uploads
  - Line 633-638: For each ready folder, call `spawn_metadata_publish()`
    - Spawns background thread to encrypt metadata + upload to IPFS + publish via IPNS
    - Logs: **"Background metadata publish succeeded for {ipns_name}"**

### **THE CRITICAL BUG: Missing drain_upload_completions() in readdir()**

**Location:** `dir_ops.rs:21-192` - `handle_readdir()` 

**Current code (line 28-29):**
```rust
fs.drain_refresh_completions();
fs.drain_filepointer_completions();
```

**Missing:**
```rust
fs.drain_upload_completions();  // <-- NOT CALLED
```

**Impact on CI/Desktop E2E Tests:**

The test sequence likely follows this pattern:
1. **Create file** → writes to FUSE mount
2. **Release/close file** → `release()` spawns async upload on background thread
3. **Immediately check folder metadata for published result** (without waiting for readdir)
   - OR: Test only calls `getattr()`/`lookup()` which don't drain uploads
   - Test never calls `readdir()`, so `drain_upload_completions()` is never invoked
4. Upload completes silently on background thread
5. `UploadComplete` message sits in channel, **never drained**
6. `flush_publish_queue()` is never called → folder metadata NEVER publishes
7. Result: **Zero publish-related log lines** → `"Background metadata publish succeeded"` never appears

### **Why It Works on Manual Testing:**

When a human:
1. Writes file to mounted volume
2. Checks file in Finder or terminal → triggers `readdir()`
3. `readdir()` would need to call `drain_upload_completions()` (if it did)
4. Metadata publishes
5. Second `readdir()` shows updated metadata

But the test probably does:
```python
# Write file
fs.write("/CipherBox/file.txt", data)
# Check logs for publish (without listing directory)
assert "Background metadata publish succeeded" in logs  # FAILS
```

### **macOS FUSE-T Specific Issues:**

**From CLAUDE.md:** FUSE-T uses SMB backend with these constraints:
- Line 247-256 in `/apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs`: `fuser::mount2()` runs FUSE callbacks on a **single thread**
- Callback blocking = entire filesystem blocking
- No event loop—sequential callback dispatch
- Each callback must be fast; async work is spawned to tokio runtime

**This means:**
- If `readdir()` never runs in the test, `drain_upload_completions()` never runs
- The tokio runtime is separate (running on different thread), so uploads proceed
- But the FUSE thread never learns about them

### **THE FIX:**

Add `drain_upload_completions()` to `dir_ops.rs:handle_readdir()`:

```rust
pub fn handle_readdir(
    fs: &mut CipherBoxFS,
    ino: u64,
    offset: i64,
    mut reply: ReplyDirectory,
) {
    // ADD THIS LINE:
    fs.drain_upload_completions();  // <-- CRITICAL: drain uploads before anything else
    
    // 1. Drain any pending background refresh results (non-blocking)
    fs.drain_refresh_completions();
    fs.drain_filepointer_completions();
    // ... rest of function
}
```

**Also consider adding to other frequently-called ops:**
- `handle_lookup()` (line 104-211 in `read_ops.rs`) — already has it at line 110 ✓
- `handle_getattr()` (line 214-227 in `read_ops.rs`) — already has it at line 219 ✓
- Any test helper that doesn't naturally call these ops needs to explicitly drain

### **File Locations with Line Numbers:**

1. **`crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:599-618`** - `drain_upload_completions()` implementation
2. **`crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:620-624`** - `queue_publish()` adds folder to publish queue
3. **`crates/fuse/src/lib.rs:626-640`** - `flush_publish_queue()` checks debounce/pending and publishes
4. **`crates/fuse/src/read_ops.rs:656-855`** - `handle_release()` spawns upload thread and sends UploadComplete
5. **`crates/fuse/src/dir_ops.rs:21-192`** - `handle_readdir()` **MISSING drain_upload_completions() call**
6. **`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:50-264`** - Mount logic, creates channel receivers
7. **`crates/fuse/src/operations.rs:203-291`** - Callback dispatch layer

The pipeline is correctly designed but has a **drainage hole** in `readdir()` that prevents the E2E test from observing published metadata.
```

99	  ROOT_IPNS=$(echo "$VAULT_RESPONSE" | jq -r '.rootIpnsName // empty' 2>/dev/null || echo "")
100	  if [ -n "$ROOT_IPNS" ] && [ "$ROOT_IPNS" != "null" ]; then
101	    break
102	  fi
103	  echo "  Vault poll attempt $attempt: no rootIpnsName yet"
```

> AGENT

Pushed and CI running — [24008805958](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/24008805958).

The root cause: after `release()` spawns the background IPFS upload, no further FUSE callbacks happen in CI (no Spotlight, no Finder). The `UploadComplete` message sits in the channel, `drain_upload_completions()` never runs, and the folder metadata publish never fires. The fix adds `drain_upload_completions()` to `readdir` (matching Windows behavior) and forces an `ls` in the test scripts to trigger it.

> AGENT

The issue is that macOS FUSE-T uses SMB backend which caches directory listings — the `ls` might not reach the FUSE layer. Let me add FUSE touches inside the polling loop to repeatedly trigger drains.

> TOOL

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/desktop-e2e/scripts/test-round-trip.sh",
  "old_string": "for attempt in $(seq 1 24); do\n  sleep 5\n  if FILE_VERIFY_OUTPUT=$(run_filepointer_verifier 2>&1); then\n    FILE_VERIFY_OK=1\n    break\n  fi\n  echo \"  FilePointer verify attempt $attempt failed: $FILE_VERIFY_OUTPUT\"\ndone",
  "new_string": "for attempt in $(seq 1 24); do\n  # Touch the FUSE mount each iteration to trigger drain_upload_completions()\n  # via lookup/getattr/readdir callbacks. FUSE-T's SMB backend may cache\n  # directory listings, so we stat the specific file to force a lookup.\n  stat \"$MP/$TEST_FILE\" > /dev/null 2>&1 || true\n  ls \"$MP\" > /dev/null 2>&1 || true\n  sleep 5\n  if FILE_VERIFY_OUTPUT=$(run_filepointer_verifier 2>&1); then\n    FILE_VERIFY_OK=1\n    break\n  fi\n  echo \"  FilePointer verify attempt $attempt failed: $FILE_VERIFY_OUTPUT\"\ndone"
}
